Building a corpus of SEC filings

Uses the SEC company index to download 100 Form 10-K filings and builds a lowercase word-frequency dictionary across the downloaded text. This creates the starting point for the financial-text analyses in the following weeks.

Download notebook (.ipynb)

Static notebook reader. Code and saved outputs are displayed without running them. Uses requests and Google Colab/Drive paths. Extract company.idx.zip and update the index/output paths before rerunning. The downloaded raw filing corpus was not supplied; the notebook contains the collection code. The dictionary and empty cleanText.txt placeholder are included as they appeared in my Week 3 folder.

Cell 1
from google.colab import drive
drive.mount('/content/drive')

idxFile = "/content/drive/MyDrive/Fall 2026 Text Mining/week 01/company.idx"
websitePrefix = "https://www.sec.gov/Archives/"
Saved output
Drive already mounted at /content/drive; to attempt to forcibly remount, call drive.mount("/content/drive", force_remount=True).
Cell 2
with open(idxFile) as IndexFile:
  text = IndexFile.readlines()
display(text[:5]) # Display the first 5 lines to confirm content
Saved output
['Description:           Master Index of EDGAR Dissemination Feed by Company Name\n',
 'Last Data Received:    March 31, 2025\n',
 'Comments:              webmaster@sec.gov\n',
 'Anonymous FTP:         ftp://ftp.sec.gov/edgar/\n',
 '\n']
Cell 3
for line in text:
  if line.find('10-K') != -1:
    print(line)

Cell 9
count = 0

for line in text:
  Attributes = line.split()

  if len(line.split()) >= 5 and line.split()[-4] == "10-K":
    URL = websitePrefix + Attributes[-1]
    fileDate = Attributes[-2]
    CIK = Attributes[-3]
    formType = Attributes[-4]
    formType.replace('/', '_')

    response = requests.get(URL, headers=headers)
    count += 1

    fileName = str(count) + "_" + formType + "_" + CIK + "_" + fileDate + ".txt"

    with open(downloadDir + fileName, "w") as f:
      f.write(response.text)

    print(count, fileName)

  if count == 100:
    break

print("Downloaded", count, "files")

Cell 10
import os
import re

word_counts = {}

for fileName in os.listdir(downloadDir):
  if fileName.endswith(".txt"):
    with open(downloadDir + fileName, "r") as f:
      content = f.read().lower()

    words = re.findall(r"[a-z]+", content)

    for word in words:
      if word in word_counts:
        word_counts[word] = word_counts[word] + 1
      else:
        word_counts[word] = 1
